- [A ticket's owner is supplied, never guessed](decisions/084-a-tickets-owner-is-supplied-never-guessed.md)
# A ticket's owner is supplied, never guessed — 2026-09-08

**Decision:** `core` does not read the environment to decide who a ticket belongs to. `Backlog.create`
takes an owner from its caller, refuses one that is not a non-empty string, and writes **`unknown`**
where none was supplied. The surface decides who that is: `quorum ticket new` uses `--owner` if it
was given a name, then git's configured `user.name`, then nothing — and M3's server will use whatever
its session knows. `owner` therefore carries **a name or the sentinel `unknown`**, which
`packages/shared/src/ticket.ts` now says where it previously said only *"the human who owns the
current stage"*.

**Why this is a decision and not a repair.** Q-0112 opened on two defects in one field, and the
second is a genuine defect by any reading: `--owner` with no value after it wrote the **boolean
`true`**, because `argv.ts:54` gives a valueless flag `true` and `create`'s destructuring default
fired only on `undefined` — while `ticketSchema` declares `owner: z.string()` and refuses that value.
The product's own command could write a ticket its own schema rejects, into the module it calls its
database, and `quorum board` rendered it without noticing. Refusing it needs no entry.

The first does. `process.env.USER` is not a wrong *value* the way `true` is; it is a **policy** —
that a ticket belongs to the operating-system account of whoever ran the command. It is the wrong
policy, on a shared machine, in CI, in a container and in every worktree a flow creates, and this
backlog carried eleven tickets stamped that way with four hand corrections that never reached the
code. Replacing it means choosing a different policy, and choosing `unknown` puts a value in a field
the schema defines as *a human*. That is the conflict `.claude/rules/docs-and-decisions.md` says an
entry has to settle before the code is right.

**Where each half lives, and why the seam is there.** `core` gained `configuredUser`, which answers
what git is configured to call this user and `null` where nothing is configured. It is a **fact**;
that this fact is the owner is a **policy**, and the policy is the surface's. That is the same seam
*"A `core` error names the condition; the remedy belongs to the surface"* (2026-09-07) drew for error
text, and it is drawn here for the same reason: `core` cannot see which surface is asking. A terminal
user has a git identity; a browser session has a logged-in account; neither is knowable from
`packages/core`.

**The refusal is at the write boundary rather than at the flag.** `argv`'s coercion is unchanged and
a valueless `--owner` still arrives as `true`; what changed is that a boolean is no longer a name.
Putting the check in `create` means one guarantee covers the CLI flag today and an owner taken from
an HTTP request body tomorrow, where a surface-by-surface check would have to be remembered twice.

**Alternatives considered.** *Require an owner and let `create` refuse without one* — the strongest
form, and rejected on what it costs a stranger: a cold clone with no git identity configured would be
unable to create a ticket at all, in the first thirty minutes the README is timed against. *Keep a
default but make it `git config user.name` inside `core`* — rejected because it is the same mistake
one layer better disguised: `core` would be choosing a policy it cannot see the consequences of, and
would owe an answer for the case where git has none. *Fall back to `unknown` only after refusing
`true`, leaving `process.env.USER` in place* — rejected: it repairs the defect that is easy to see
and keeps the one that has already cost four hand corrections.

**What `unknown` claims, stated so it is not read as a name.** It is an admission that nobody said,
not an assertion about a person, and it is visible on `quorum board` beside every other owner — which
is the point. A surface that forgets to supply an owner ships tickets that say so, loudly, rather
than tickets attributed to a plausible-looking account nobody chose.
# Glossary — Quorum

**Harness**: The complete agentic development flow of a project, from start to finish, expressed as versioned configuration — rules/CLAUDE.md, slash commands, subagents, skills, MCP servers, permissions, plus the orchestration patterns that combine agents (e.g. two coder agents + one judge, multi-model code review). Lives in the project folder (`.claude/` and friends), not in the UI's database.

**Quorum**: The local web app defined here — a visual workbench to create, maintain, execute and observe harnesses. Named 2026-08-22; DECISIONS entries written before that date call it **the Studio**, which is the same thing and is not current vocabulary.

**Agent-agnostic**: Able to orchestrate any coding agent that ships as a headless CLI with its own subscription login (Claude Code, Codex CLI, Gemini CLI, …) via a common adapter interface. Does NOT mean direct API integration with model vendors.

**BYOS (bring your own subscriptions)**: The auth model — Quorum never stores or uses API keys; every agent runs on the OAuth login of the CLI the user already pays for.

**Canonical harness**: The single per-project source of truth (`harness/` folder): rules, architecture context, command prompts, flow files. Compiled by Quorum into vendor dialects (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, GEMINI.md); vendor-unique features pass through in marked native sections.

**Gate**: A checkpoint in a flow. An author-declared gate is human-gated by default and may be set to `auto`; an author-declared `human-locked` deploy gate can never be automated. Separately, an engine-presented exhaustion gate appears when a bounded loop exhausts. It uses the same gate kind but is not declared as a flow step, requires an explicit `advance`, `retry`, or `abort`, and cannot be bypassed by `--auto`.

**Flow**: A declarative, git-versioned file in the harness describing one orchestration: ordered steps, which adapter+model runs each step, what each step receives, and the gates between steps. Example: "grill → 2 competing coders → judge → reviewer panel". Since 2026-08-21 a flow also declares the backlog stage it `consumes` and `produces`.

**Template library**: Flows that ship with Quorum as starting points, encoding the opinionated SDLC (grill, architecture, development, QA, maintenance). Users copy and adapt them; nothing is enforced.

**Adapter**: The thin integration layer that lets one CLI agent participate in Quorum: launch headless, stream events, map its output to Quorum's common trace format, stop/abort.

**Backlog**: The per-project (or central, multi-repo) folder of ticket folders in git. Replaces Jira. Its `stage` fields drive which flow can run next.

**Ticket**: One folder in the backlog: `ticket.md` (frontmatter state + intent) and per-stage artifact subfolders (requirements/, solution/, qa/, dev/, review/, deploy/). Its **id** is `<PREFIX>-nnnn`, the grammar `/^[A-Z]+-[0-9]{4}$/` that `quorum runs <token>` already resolves against, and the prefix belongs to the adopter rather than to the product: `quorum ticket new` allocates within the one prefix the backlog's tickets already carry, so a `PROJ-0041` backlog allocates `PROJ-0042` with no configuration step. An **empty** backlog allocates `T-0001`, which is the id `quorum init` prints as its own next command. A backlog the allocator **cannot read** — no id parses, more than one prefix does, or the prefix is full at `9999` — refuses and names what it found, rather than answering; `--id <ID>` allocates explicitly through the same checks, and `create` refuses a taken id or an occupied folder instead of overwriting one. Reading is not constrained by any of this: every id on disk still lists, reads and resolves, whatever its prefix.

**Stage**: The ticket's position in the SDLC state machine (draft → requirements → solutioned → red → green → reviewed → qa-passed → deployed, plus blocked/abandoned). Flows `consume` one stage and `produce` a later one — usually the next, though the **chore flow** produces `reviewed` from `requirements`. `green` means the ticket's integration branch integrated and passed its configured suite; no stage — `green` or any later one — implies the branch is contained in the base branch. Where the code actually is appears on `quorum board` as **Containment**.

**Containment**: The git-derived relationship between a ticket branch tip and the configured base
branch, computed on every `quorum board` invocation and never stored. Exactly three states,
rendered as one token beside the ticket: contained (`main:contained` — the branch tip is an
ancestor of the base tip), not contained (`main:not-contained(+12)` — with the count of commits
reachable from the branch and not from the base), and indeterminate (`main:indeterminate(missing
ref)`, `main:indeterminate(shallow clone)`, `main:indeterminate(git failed)`, `main:indeterminate(no
branch)` — the board could not answer, which is never reported as either of the other two). The
first three are git declining to answer; `no branch` is the ticket naming a branch that does not
exist, so git was never asked. Every ticket names one from creation and most never have one, so the
board renders `no branch` only where the stage claims the work is done and the branch is the
evidence for it — `solutioned` onward, never `draft`, `requirements`, `blocked` or `abandoned`.
An ancestry fact about two refs at the moment of reading, not a claim about how the code arrived —
and not a synonym-carrier: the board and the docs say "contained", never "merged", "landed" or
"shipped".

**Push lag**: The number of commits the configured base branch holds that its configured upstream
tracking ref does not — `<upstream>..<base>`, never the symmetric difference — computed on every
`quorum board` invocation and never stored. **Containment**'s sibling and under the same rules: one
repository-level fact rather than a per-ticket annotation, derived from git alone, selected from a
closed set of states, and never reported as an answer git did not give. Five of those states are
reasons the board could not answer — `no remote` (the repository has no remotes at all), `no
upstream` (a remote exists and the base tracks nothing), `missing ref` (the base branch, or the
tracking ref its configuration names, does not resolve — an upstream can be named and gone, which
git renders as `[gone]`), `shallow clone` (truncated history can only make the count too small) and
`git failed` (the probe could not answer, which is not the same as git answering that there is no
repository here — that is silence) — of which `no remote` renders nothing,
because a repository with nowhere to push has nothing to be ahead of. Rendered as at most one dim
legend line naming the base, its upstream and the count, and **never as a `<base>:` token**, which
is containment's grammar. **The line may warn and may never reassure**: a `git push` updates the
tracking ref locally, so absent a fetch the count can only over-report, and silence means no more
than *git answered and there is nothing to say*. Not containment (a different pair of refs answering
a different question), not "behind" (the opposite direction, which is not push lag at all), not "out
of date", and **not a claim that anything was built, tested or validated anywhere** — it says that
commits have not left this machine and nothing else, which is why no rendered state, legend or help
text may use wording equivalent to "CI passed" or "not validated". See *"The board reports push lag,
and never a CI conclusion"* (2026-09-06), which extends the 2026-08-24 containment entry rather than
contradicting it.

**Contract**: A machine-checkable artifact emitted by solutioning — interface, schema, stub, migration skeleton — that tests and developers code against.

**Role**: An agent persona file in `harness/roles/` with default adapter, model, write-path allow-list and prompt (product-manager, principal-architect, developer-backend, code-reviewer, …). Tasks reference roles; flows reference roles.

**Backward edge**: An `on_fail: goto` from a step/route to an earlier step or another flow, always with `max_iterations` and `on_exhausted: gate`.

**Fan-out step**: A step that expands `tasks.yaml` into N parallel worktree steps, one per task, grouped by role.

**Integrate step**: Merges the fan-out branches onto the ticket branch and runs the test suite; failure loops back to the failing tasks only.

**Cross-vendor rule**: `cross_vendor: required` — a lint guaranteeing the reviewer/judge of an artifact runs on a different adapter than its writer.

**Panel**: A parallel group of reviewing or judging steps over the same input, spanning more than one
adapter. The review flow's Claude + Codex reviewers are a panel; `cross_vendor: required` is satisfied
by the panel spanning vendors, not by writer ≠ reviewer.

**Human-locked gate**: A gate that cannot be flipped to `auto` (deploy).

**Event**: One item of the trace a run emits, defined once in `packages/shared` as a discriminated
union on `type`. Two shapes, because two interfaces exist. An **adapter event** is what an adapter
passes to `onEvent` — `spawn`, `stdout`, and the `retry` the contract layer adds — carrying no
identity, because an adapter does not know which step it is running. A **run event** is one of
those with the step id the engine supplies, or one of the engine's own: `step`, `done`, `info`,
`warn`, the correlated gate question, and the final `terminal` member. The gate question is queued
before the out-of-band `answerGate` callback is invoked. `runFlow` is a lazy, single-consumer
`AsyncIterable<Event>` backed by a lossless FIFO: order is stable within one step, but parallel
members have no global ordering or interleaving promise. Cancellation belongs to the caller through
an `AbortSignal`, not to a signal handler installed by core. No event gains a timestamp or sequence
number, and only the terminal event carries run identity. Vendor identity is one neutral, open
`vendor` label and nothing else in the union is vendor-specific. Not persisted in v1 (see **Run
history**, which is), and not to be called a "log line" or a "trace message" — the trace is the
stream, an event is one item of it. See *What a run's event stream carries, and how a gate answer travels back* (2026-08-28) and its 2026-08-29 erratum, which corrects two of its clauses.

**Run history**: The durable record of one run under `.quorum/runs/`: its manifest, per-attempt
prompts and outputs, errors, usage, and per-vendor roll-up.

**Undecided**: A run's terminal status, never a gate answer — the answers a gate accepts are still
exactly `advance`, `retry` and `abort`. A run is undecided when it stops at a gate for which **no
answer was available**: the scripted answers were exhausted and stdin is not a terminal, stdin
closed while the question was open, or the caller supplied no answer channel. It moves no stage and
keeps every worktree the run obtained, like the other non-advancing statuses, and alone among them
it does **not** restore the ticket branch — nothing was proved wrong, so the merge an `integrate`
step had already proven green survives. Somebody who supplied a word that is not an answer *was*
there: that is an operator error and stays `failed`, rollback included. The CLI exits **3**. It is
terminal rather than suspended — nothing is held open, and M3's resume (Q-0019) may later choose it
as the state it picks up. Not a synonym for "aborted", "failed" or "paused". See *"A run nobody
answered is undecided, and keeps the branch it proved"* (2026-09-01).

**Occurrence**: One entry in a run manifest's record of what actually executed — an adapter call, a
script, or an integrate step — carrying its own usage, errors and retained files. Adapter
occurrences keep their exact `prompt.txt` and `output.txt`; gates and fan-out parents allocate
none. The unit a roll-up sums over.

**Preflight**: A check a run performs before invoking any adapter, so that bad evidence is found
before it is paid for. The run-level diff preflight materialises every range whose endpoints all
exist at run start, and its guarantee is **per endpoint, not per range**: where an endpoint is one
an earlier step of the flow has yet to create, the range is deferred to step time and every
endpoint beside it that is already due is still resolved at run start, so a branch nobody created
stops the run before anything is spent. It never resolves an endpoint that is not due, and never
reports one as having failed to resolve. A preflight that declines to examine something reports that it *skipped*
it — reporting success for an unexamined subject is the failure recorded in the 2026-08-25
decision.

**Base override** (`--base <ref>`): `quorum run … --base <ref>` aims a run's `{base}` at an
arbitrary revision instead of `repo.base_branch`. It moves the **diff anchor** only — what a review
compares the ticket's work against — and never the branch a rework step or `integrate` merges from,
which keeps reading the configured base. Without it a ticket whose branch is contained in the base
has an empty review range and cannot be reviewed at all, which is why a merged ticket needed a
hand-run review before Q-0077. Not a way to change what a run writes.

**Dry run** (`--dry`): `quorum run … --dry` walks a flow without invoking an adapter or writing
anything, reporting what each step would do. It is the same run machinery, not a separate code
path, which is why its preflight must be as honest as a real run's. Not called a "preview" —
DECISIONS entries before 2026-08-25 use that word for it.

**Chore flow**: The short route for machinery and configuration tickets — requirements → one implementer
in a worktree → cross-vendor review with a bounded revise loop → integrate → human gate. Consumes
`requirements`, produces `reviewed`, skipping solutioning and qa-red because a scaffold has no
behaviour a test could fail on before it exists. Not a lighter SDLC; a different one, for work that
changes what the repository *is* rather than what it *does*. Requires `harness/<id>/integration` to
exist before its first run — `review` diffs against that branch and only `integrate`, which runs
later, creates it (see 02-sdlc-pipeline-spec.md §5.8).

**Build task**: Turborepo's `build` task, declared once in the root `turbo.json` and run in the
three packages that emit. It is the **first task in this workspace whose `outputs` is non-empty**,
so a cache hit on it replays an *artifact* where `lint`, `typecheck` and `test` replay only a
verdict — the distinction the 2026-09-02 decision rests on. Not a "pipeline", a "job" or a "step":
a pipeline is turbo's own retired name for the task table, a job is one of CI's seven, and a step is
a flow's. Nothing in `harness/flows/` runs it, and no test verdict in this workspace moves behind
it: the suites resolve TypeScript source through the `quorum-source` export condition, and the emit
is what Node and a packed install get. See *"The emit serves the binary, and no test verdict moves
behind it"* (2026-09-02).

**Emitted artifact**: The JavaScript and declaration files a **build task** writes under a package's
`dist/`, gitignored and reproducible from the commit. The three emitting packages are
`@quorum/shared`, `@quorum/core` and `@quorum/cli`, which is also the **local distribution set** —
what a `pnpm pack` of this repository produces and what an installation outside the workspace
consumes. Distinguished from the **binary**, which is the single file `packages/cli`'s `bin.quorum`
names: the binary is one emitted artifact among many and the two words are not interchangeable. An
emitted artifact is not a "bundle" — nothing here is bundled, each source file emits its own
counterpart — and not a "build output directory", which names the container rather than the
contents. Since Q-0098 the binary carries a shebang and an executable bit, both proven to survive a
cache replay, because an artifact something *executes* fails differently from a stale tick: the tick
lies about the past, the artifact lies about the present.

docs/decisions/084-a-tickets-owner-is-supplied-never-guessed.md
     1	# A ticket's owner is supplied, never guessed — 2026-09-08
     2	
     3	**Decision:** `core` does not read the environment to decide who a ticket belongs to. `Backlog.create`
     4	takes an owner from its caller, refuses one that is not a non-empty string, and writes **`unknown`**
     5	where none was supplied. The surface decides who that is: `quorum ticket new` uses `--owner` if it
     6	was given a name, then git's configured `user.name`, then nothing — and M3's server will use whatever
     7	its session knows. `owner` therefore carries **a name or the sentinel `unknown`**, which
     8	`packages/shared/src/ticket.ts` now says where it previously said only *"the human who owns the
     9	current stage"*.
    10	
    11	**Why this is a decision and not a repair.** Q-0112 opened on two defects in one field, and the
    12	second is a genuine defect by any reading: `--owner` with no value after it wrote the **boolean
    13	`true`**, because `argv.ts:54` gives a valueless flag `true` and `create`'s destructuring default
    14	fired only on `undefined` — while `ticketSchema` declares `owner: z.string()` and refuses that value.
    15	The product's own command could write a ticket its own schema rejects, into the module it calls its
    16	database, and `quorum board` rendered it without noticing. Refusing it needs no entry.
    17	
    18	The first does. `process.env.USER` is not a wrong *value* the way `true` is; it is a **policy** —
    19	that a ticket belongs to the operating-system account of whoever ran the command. It is the wrong
    20	policy, on a shared machine, in CI, in a container and in every worktree a flow creates, and this
    21	backlog carried eleven tickets stamped that way with four hand corrections that never reached the
    22	code. Replacing it means choosing a different policy, and choosing `unknown` puts a value in a field
    23	the schema defines as *a human*. That is the conflict `.claude/rules/docs-and-decisions.md` says an
    24	entry has to settle before the code is right.
    25	
    26	**Where each half lives, and why the seam is there.** `core` gained `configuredUser`, which answers
    27	what git is configured to call this user and `null` where nothing is configured. It is a **fact**;
    28	that this fact is the owner is a **policy**, and the policy is the surface's. That is the same seam
    29	*"A `core` error names the condition; the remedy belongs to the surface"* (2026-09-07) drew for error
    30	text, and it is drawn here for the same reason: `core` cannot see which surface is asking. A terminal
    31	user has a git identity; a browser session has a logged-in account; neither is knowable from
    32	`packages/core`.
    33	
    34	**The refusal is at the write boundary rather than at the flag.** `argv`'s coercion is unchanged and
    35	a valueless `--owner` still arrives as `true`; what changed is that a boolean is no longer a name.
    36	Putting the check in `create` means one guarantee covers the CLI flag today and an owner taken from
    37	an HTTP request body tomorrow, where a surface-by-surface check would have to be remembered twice.
    38	
    39	**Alternatives considered.** *Require an owner and let `create` refuse without one* — the strongest
    40	form, and rejected on what it costs a stranger: a cold clone with no git identity configured would be
    41	unable to create a ticket at all, in the first thirty minutes the README is timed against. *Keep a
    42	default but make it `git config user.name` inside `core`* — rejected because it is the same mistake
    43	one layer better disguised: `core` would be choosing a policy it cannot see the consequences of, and
    44	would owe an answer for the case where git has none. *Fall back to `unknown` only after refusing
    45	`true`, leaving `process.env.USER` in place* — rejected: it repairs the defect that is easy to see
    46	and keeps the one that has already cost four hand corrections.
    47	
    48	**What `unknown` claims, stated so it is not read as a name.** It is an admission that nobody said,
    49	not an assertion about a person, and it is visible on `quorum board` beside every other owner — which
    50	is the point. A surface that forgets to supply an owner ships tickets that say so, loudly, rather
    51	than tickets attributed to a plausible-looking account nobody chose.
     1	/**
     2	 * Worktrees, ancestry and containment. Never writes to the user's working tree.
     3	 *
     4	 * Why: behaviour preserved from spike/src/git.js — harness/port-charter.md §2, Q-0042.
     5	 */
     6	import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';
     7	import fs from 'node:fs';
     8	import path from 'node:path';
     9	
    10	import { REPO_WORKTREE_ROOT, worktreeDirName } from '@quorum/shared';
    11	import type {
    12	  AncestryReason, AncestryResult, ContainmentReason, ContainmentResult, PushLagResult,
    13	} from '@quorum/shared';
    14	
    15	/** Every git call: argv, never a shell — branch names carry agent-written task ids (Q-0011). */
    16	const git = (args: readonly string[], cwd: string): string =>
    17	  execFileSync('git', args, { cwd, encoding: 'utf8', stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'] }).trim();
    18	
    19	const safe = <T>(fn: () => T): T | null => {
    20	  try { return fn(); } catch { return null; }
    21	};
    22	
    23	/**
    24	 * One property off whatever `execFileSync` threw — `undefined` when it is absent or of another
    25	 * type, so no caller mistakes a missing property for a convenient default.
    26	 */
    27	function errorProperty(error: unknown, key: 'status' | 'stderr' | 'message'): unknown {
    28	  return typeof error === 'object' && error !== null && key in error
    29	    ? (error as Record<string, unknown>)[key]
    30	    : undefined;
    31	}
    32	
    33	/**
    34	 * The child's exit status, or `null` when the throw carried none. {@link ancestry} turns on this
    35	 * being exactly `1`, so anything that is not a number is not a `1`.
    36	 */
    37	const exitStatus = (error: unknown): number | null => {
    38	  const status = errorProperty(error, 'status');
    39	  return typeof status === 'number' ? status : null;
    40	};
    41	
    42	/**
    43	 * git's exit code for a fatal, and it spends the same one on every kind. Measured on git 2.55:
    44	 * `not a git repository`, `Expected git repo version <= 1, found 99`, `unknown repository extension
    45	 * found` and `detected dubious ownership in repository at …` all exit 128. So the code says *git
    46	 * gave up* and never why, which is what {@link repositoryAt} is for.
    47	 */
    48	const GIT_FATAL = 128;
    49	
    50	/** What the work-tree probe established, which is three answers rather than a boolean. */
    51	type WorkTreeProbe =
    52	  /** git answered `true`: there is a work tree here and it can be asked questions. */
    53	  | 'inside'
    54	  /** git established there is no work tree to ask about — it answered `false`, or it proved absence. */
    55	  | 'outside'
    56	  /** The probe could not answer, which is never reported as either of the other two. */
    57	  | 'failed';
    58	
    59	/**
    60	 * Does a repository sit at `repoDir` — one git found and then refused to open?
    61	 *
    62	 * `--resolve-git-dir` asks whether a *path* is a gitdir, or a gitfile naming one, and it is the
    63	 * only probe measured here that answers while the repository is unopenable: on git 2.55 it exits 0
    64	 * for a repository whose format version is 99, for one carrying an unknown extension and for one
    65	 * whose ownership git refuses, and 128 where there is no gitdir at that path. It runs neither the
    66	 * ownership check nor the format check, which is precisely why it can discriminate between them and
    67	 * absence — and it is git's own answer rather than a guess read off git's prose, which is
    68	 * translated and so may never decide a state.
    69	 */
    70	function repositoryAt(repoDir: string): boolean {
    71	  return safe(() => git(['rev-parse', '--resolve-git-dir', path.join(repoDir, '.git')], repoDir)) != null;
    72	}
    73	
    74	/**
    75	 * Is `repoDir` inside a work tree? Three answers, because a probe that could not answer is a
    76	 * different thing from either of git's own and is never collapsed into one of them.
    77	 *
    78	 * The distinction is what {@link pushLag} needs and {@link containment} does not. Anything that is
    79	 * not {@link GIT_FATAL} — no git on the path, a killed process, a shim — is the probe failing. A
    80	 * fatal is git giving up for one of two very different reasons, so it is asked a second question:
    81	 * where a repository is really there, git refused to open one and that is a failed probe *inside*
    82	 * the subject, which for a fact whose success output is silence must not be rendered as nothing.
    83	 *
    84	 * The residual limit, stated rather than hidden and narrower than it was: a repository git refuses
    85	 * is caught where it sits at `repoDir`, which is where a project's own `.git` is. A project root
    86	 * *below* the refused repository's root still reads as absence, because closing that needs either
    87	 * git's translated prose or a reimplementation of git's upward discovery walk — and the second would
    88	 * make a fixture's verdict depend on whether the directory it was built in happens to sit under a
    89	 * repository, which is the one thing a verdict may not turn on (2026-08-30).
    90	 */
    91	function workTreeProbe(repoDir: string): WorkTreeProbe {
    92	  try { return git(['rev-parse', '--is-inside-work-tree'], repoDir) === 'true' ? 'inside' : 'outside'; }
    93	  catch (error) {
    94	    if (exitStatus(error) !== GIT_FATAL) return 'failed';
    95	    return repositoryAt(repoDir) ? 'failed' : 'outside';
    96	  }
    97	}
    98	
    99	/**
   100	 * Does `ref` name a commit in this repository? `false` on git's own "no such ref" exit of 1, which
   101	 * `--verify --quiet` is documented to use; `null` where the probe failed for any other reason, so a
   102	 * broken git is never reported as an absent ref.
   103	 */
   104	function resolvesToCommit(repoDir: string, ref: string): boolean | null {
   105	  try { git(['rev-parse', '--verify', '--quiet', `${ref}^{commit}`], repoDir); return true; }
   106	  catch (error) { return exitStatus(error) === 1 ? false : null; }
   107	}
   108	
   109	const firstLine = (text: unknown): string | null => {
   110	  const line = String(text ?? '').split('\n').map((l) => l.trim()).filter(Boolean)[0];
   111	  return line ? line.slice(0, 200) : null;
   112	};
   113	
   114	/** git's own first line of stderr, falling back to the error's message, normalised and truncated. */
   115	const failureDetail = (error: unknown): string | null =>
   116	  firstLine(errorProperty(error, 'stderr')) ?? firstLine(errorProperty(error, 'message'));
   117	
   118	/**
   119	 * The pattern appended to the repository's own `info/exclude`, so the worktree root never shows up
   120	 * in the user's `git status`.
   121	 *
   122	 * Why: a literal rather than `TICKET_ARTIFACT_DIR` — that is the other `.harness` namespace and
   123	 * lacks the trailing slash. This string is written into a file in the user's repository, which
   124	 * makes it externally observable and means it must survive byte for byte.
   125	 */
   126	const EXCLUDE_PATTERN = '.harness/';
   127	
   128	/**
   129	 * The worktree for `branch` under the repository's worktree root, created if it is not there. An
   130	 * existing directory is returned untouched; an absent branch is created from `base` when `base`
   131	 * resolves, and from `HEAD` otherwise.
   132	 */
   133	export function ensureWorktree(repoDir: string, branch: string, base?: string | null): string {
   134	  const root = path.join(repoDir, REPO_WORKTREE_ROOT);
   135	  const dir = path.join(root, worktreeDirName(branch));
   136	  if (fs.existsSync(dir)) return dir;
   137	  fs.mkdirSync(root, { recursive: true });
   138	  ensureExcluded(repoDir, EXCLUDE_PATTERN);
   139	  const branchExists = safe(() => git(['rev-parse', '--verify', '--quiet', `refs/heads/${branch}`], repoDir));
   140	  if (branchExists) {
   141	    git(['worktree', 'add', dir, branch], repoDir);
   142	  } else {
   143	    const baseExists = base ? safe(() => git(['rev-parse', '--verify', '--quiet', `refs/heads/${base}`], repoDir)) : null;
   144	    git(['worktree', 'add', '-b', branch, dir, base && baseExists ? base : 'HEAD'], repoDir);
   145	  }
   146	  return dir;
   147	}
   148	
   149	/** Remove `branch`'s worktree if it is there, and the branch too when `deleteBranch` says so. */
   150	export function removeWorktree(
   151	  repoDir: string,
   152	  branch: string,
   153	  { deleteBranch = false }: { deleteBranch?: boolean } = {},
   154	): void {
   155	  const dir = path.join(repoDir, REPO_WORKTREE_ROOT, worktreeDirName(branch));
   156	  if (fs.existsSync(dir)) git(['worktree', 'remove', '--force', dir], repoDir);
   157	  if (deleteBranch) safe(() => git(['branch', '-D', branch], repoDir));
   158	}
   159	
   160	/**
   161	 * Where `a` and `b` forked, as a sha, or `null` when git could not answer — a ref that does not
   162	 * exist being one of the reasons, which this deliberately does not tell apart from the others.
   163	 *
   164	 * It lives here rather than in the engine because a landed guard permits `merge-base` in exactly one
   165	 * file, and {@link ancestry} is not a substitute: that one asks a yes/no/unknown question and this
   166	 * one is read for the revision it names, in an integration step's evidence line.
   167	 *
   168	 * Why: deliberate addition, not preservation — see Q-0053 errata E-1, which accepts the one
   169	 * divergence it carries. The spike's `safeMergeBase` runs with git's stderr inherited, so a failing
   170	 * call printed git's own `fatal:` line to the terminal; this module has one runner, it pipes, and a
   171	 * library M3's daemon hosts has no terminal to print to.
   172	 */
   173	export function mergeBase(repoDir: string, a: string, b: string): string | null {
   174	  return safe(() => git(['merge-base', a, b], repoDir));
   175	}
   176	
   177	/**
   178	 * The branch `dir` is on, or `null` where git cannot name one.
   179	 *
   180	 * `git branch --show-current` names the branch even on an **unborn HEAD** — a fresh
   181	 * `git init -b <name>` before the first commit — and prints an **empty string**, not an error, on a
   182	 * detached HEAD. Both are "cannot name a branch" for a caller's purposes, so the empty string
   183	 * becomes `null` here rather than reaching one as a branch called nothing. Outside a repository, or
   184	 * with a broken `GIT_DIR`, the command itself fails and that is `null` too.
   185	 *
   186	 * **git's stderr never leaves this function**, which is a property rather than an accident of the
   187	 * runner: its one caller scaffolds a project, which is the first command an adopter runs, and a raw
   188	 * `fatal: not a git repository` on that path reads as the product having crashed. Why: preserved from
   189	 * `spike/bin/harness.js:287–292`, whose `stdio` is `['ignore', 'pipe', 'ignore']` for the same
   190	 * reason; this module's one runner already pipes both, so the behaviour is the same and the
   191	 * spelling is the module's.
   192	 *
   193	 * Not on `@quorum/core`'s barrel: a symbol reaches it because a command needs it, and no command
   194	 * asks this question — `init` asks for a project to be scaffolded and this is how that is answered.
   195	 */
   196	/**
   197	 * The name git is configured to attribute work to here, or `null` where it is not configured.
   198	 *
   199	 * A **fact and not a policy**: this says what git answers, and whether that is who a ticket belongs
   200	 * to is the caller's to decide. `null` rather than a fallback, because an absent identity is the
   201	 * state the git-identity sweep runs in and a caller that substitutes something must do it knowingly.
   202	 */
   203	export function configuredUser(dir: string): string | null {
   204	  const name = safe(() => git(['config', 'user.name'], dir))?.trim();
   205	  return name ? name : null;
   206	}
   207	
   208	/**
   209	 * The branch a checkout is on, or `null` where it is detached or git could not say.
   210	 */
   211	export function currentBranch(dir: string): string | null {
   212	  const name = safe(() => git(['branch', '--show-current'], dir));
   213	  return name === null || name === '' ? null : name;
   214	}
   215	
   216	/** What a caller already knows about the repository's history when it asks {@link ancestry}. */
   217	export interface AncestryOptions {
   218	  /** `true` shallow, `false` not shallow, `null` the probe could not answer — see {@link ancestry}. */
   219	  shallow?: boolean | null;
   220	  /** The probe's own failure detail, carried through when `shallow` is `null`. */
   221	  shallowDetail?: string | null;
   222	}
   223	
   224	/**
   225	 * Is `ref` contained in `inRef`? The repository's one answer — the board and the engine's
   226	 * empty-range diagnostic both reach it, so the two cannot drift apart.
   227	 *
   228	 * The state is selected from git's own exit code and from nothing else: 0 contained, 1 provably not
   229	 * contained, any other exit — including a git that could not run at all — indeterminate. In a
   230	 * shallow repository an exit 1 is indeterminate too, because absent history cannot disprove
   231	 * ancestry; `shallow: null`, an unanswered probe, is held distinct from `false` for the same reason.
   232	 *
   233	 * Why: "not contained" is never inferred from a failure — the containment decision of 2026-08-24,
   234	 * and Q-0035, which removed an engine-side `catch { return false }` that did.
   235	 *
     1	// Q-0042: worktrees, ancestry and containment, asserted against real git.
     2	//
     3	// The independent witness here is git itself, not the spike's suite — both suites can be green
     4	// over a wrong port (harness/port-charter.md §2), because a test ported alongside a mis-ported
     5	// module agrees with it. So every case below builds the repository, the topology and the shallow
     6	// state it asserts, and no case asserts the containment state of a branch in THIS repository.
     7	import fs from 'node:fs';
     8	import path from 'node:path';
     9	
    10	import { afterAll, describe, expect, test, vi } from 'vitest';
    11	
    12	import type { PushLagResult } from '@quorum/shared';
    13	
    14	import {
    15	  ancestry, configuredUser, containment, emptyRangeEvidence, ensureExcluded, ensureWorktree, mergeBase, pushLag,
    16	  removeWorktree, shallowState, shortSha,
    17	} from './git.js';
    18	import {
    19	  commit, commitAll, counting, git, installGitShim, notARepo, removeTempDirs, repo, shallowCloneOf,
    20	  tempDir, walk, write,
    21	} from '../../test/repo.js';
    22	
    23	afterAll(removeTempDirs);
    24	
    25	/** `feature` forked before `main` moved on: feature is contained in main, main is not in feature. */
    26	function forked(): string {
    27	  const dir = repo();
    28	  git(dir, 'branch', 'feature');
    29	  commit(dir, 'main moves on');
    30	  return dir;
    31	}
    32	
    33	const TICKET_BRANCH = 'harness/T-1/integration';
    34	
    35	/** A repository whose ticket branch exists and is contained in `main`. */
    36	function withTicketBranch(): string {
    37	  const dir = repo();
    38	  git(dir, 'branch', TICKET_BRANCH);
    39	  return dir;
    40	}
    41	
    42	describe('AC-2 — ancestry selects its state from git\'s exit code and from nothing else', () => {
    43	  test('exit 0 is contained, and every result carries all four keys', () => {
    44	    expect(ancestry(forked(), 'feature', 'main')).toStrictEqual({
    45	      state: 'contained',
    46	      reason: null,
    47	      detail: null,
    48	      command: 'git merge-base --is-ancestor feature main',
    49	    });
    50	  });
    51	
    52	  test('exit 0 stays contained whatever the shallow state — found history is real', () => {
    53	    const dir = forked();
    54	    for (const shallow of [true, false, null] as const) {
    55	      expect(ancestry(dir, 'feature', 'main', { shallow }).state, `shallow: ${shallow}`).toBe('contained');
    56	    }
    57	  });
    58	
    59	  test('exit 1 in a repository known not to be shallow is provably not contained', () => {
    60	    expect(ancestry(forked(), 'main', 'feature')).toStrictEqual({
    61	      state: 'not-contained',
    62	      reason: null,
    63	      detail: null,
    64	      command: 'git merge-base --is-ancestor main feature',
    65	    });
    66	  });
    67	
    68	  test('exit 1 in a shallow repository is indeterminate — absent history cannot disprove ancestry', () => {
    69	    expect(ancestry(forked(), 'main', 'feature', { shallow: true })).toStrictEqual({
    70	      state: 'indeterminate',
    71	      reason: 'shallow clone',
    72	      detail: null,
    73	      command: 'git merge-base --is-ancestor main feature',
    74	    });
    75	  });
    76	
    77	  test('exit 1 with an unanswered shallow probe is indeterminate, and carries the probe\'s detail', () => {
    78	    const result = ancestry(forked(), 'main', 'feature', {
    79	      shallow: null,
    80	      shallowDetail: 'fatal: not a git repository',
    81	    });
    82	    expect(result.state).toBe('indeterminate');
    83	    expect(result.reason).toBe('shallow state unknown');
    84	    expect(result.detail).toBe('fatal: not a git repository');
    85	  });
    86	
    87	  test('any other exit, and a git that could not answer at all, are indeterminate — never not-contained', () => {
    88	    for (const [what, result] of [
    89	      ['a ref that does not resolve', ancestry(forked(), 'no/such/ref', 'main')],
    90	      ['a directory that is not a repository', ancestry(notARepo(), 'main', 'main')],
   900	  });
   901	
   902	  test('it costs seven spawns, and that is constant in the number of tickets', () => {
   903	    // The measured half of `containment`'s revised budget sentence: 2n + 3 for the board's rows,
   904	    // plus this, which does not move when n does. Pinned exactly rather than bounded, so that a
   905	    // probe added or removed moves the number the JSDoc states instead of hiding inside a ceiling
   906	    // — the budget is a sentence somebody reads, and a sentence nothing checks goes stale.
   907	    const dir = tracking({ local: 2 });
   908	    const { calls } = counting(() => pushLag(dir, 'trunk'));
   909	    expect(calls, 'the longest path costs what the JSDoc says it costs').toBe(7);
   910	    expect(counting(() => pushLag(repo(), 'main')).calls,
   911	      'the cheap answers must not cost more than the expensive one').toBeLessThanOrEqual(7);
   912	  });
   913	});
   914	
   915	describe('Q-0112 — configuredUser answers what git is configured to call this user, and nothing else', () => {
   916	  /** A repository whose `user.name` is exactly `name`; `repo()` sets none of its own. */
   917	  function named(name: string): string {
   918	    const dir = repo();
   919	    git(dir, 'config', 'user.name', name);
   920	    return dir;
   921	  }
   922	
   923	  test('a configured name is returned as it is written', () => {
   924	    expect(configuredUser(named('Ada Lovelace'))).toBe('Ada Lovelace');
   925	  });
   926	
   927	  test('surrounding whitespace is trimmed, because a name is what git holds and not how it is spaced', () => {
   928	    expect(configuredUser(named('  Ada  '))).toBe('Ada');
   929	  });
   930	
   931	  test('a name that is only whitespace is NOT a name, and reads as unconfigured', () => {
   932	    // The clause `'' || null` would miss: git stores the value, `--get` returns it, and a caller
   933	    // that took it would attribute a ticket to a blank string.
   934	    expect(configuredUser(named('   '))).toBeNull();
   935	  });
   936	
   937	  /**
   938	   * The same isolation `.github/scripts/git-identity-sweep.sh` uses, and for the same reason.
   939	   *
   940	   * Unsetting `user.name` in the repository is NOT enough — `git config user.name` falls back to the
   941	   * global file, so the first draft of the two tests below asserted `null` and got this developer's
   942	   * own name. That is the behaviour we want (git's answer, wherever git found it) and a test premise
   943	   * that was simply wrong, which running it is what caught.
   944	   */
   945	  function withNoConfiguredIdentity<T>(body: () => T): T {
   946	    vi.stubEnv('GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL', path.join(tempDir('q0112-absent-'), 'nothing-here'));
   947	    vi.stubEnv('GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM', '/dev/null');
   948	    try { return body(); } finally { vi.unstubAllEnvs(); }
   949	  }
   950	
   951	  test('a name configured nowhere at all is null rather than a fallback', () => {
   952	    const dir = repo();
   953	    expect(withNoConfiguredIdentity(() => configuredUser(dir))).toBeNull();
   954	  });
   955	
   956	  test('and a directory that is not a repository is null too, rather than throwing', () => {
   957	    // `ticket.ts` calls this on the way to writing a ticket, so a throw here would turn "git has no
   958	    // opinion" into a crash on the command's own path. Isolated for the same reason: outside a
   959	    // repository git still answers from the global file.
   960	    expect(withNoConfiguredIdentity(() => configuredUser(notARepo()))).toBeNull();
   961	  });
   962	});
     1	/**
     2	 * `quorum ticket new "<title>"` — one ticket folder, at the id the backlog it is standing in comes
     3	 * to next.
     4	 *
     5	 * **The allocation is `core`'s and is not re-specified here.** `Backlog.nextId` reads the one prefix
     6	 * the backlog's tickets already carry and refuses rather than guessing when it cannot read one;
     7	 * `Backlog.create` refuses a taken id and an occupied folder rather than allocating around either.
     8	 * That table is Q-0080's, it is asserted from `packages/core/src/backlog/q0080-allocation.json` —
     9	 * the one copy, moved there by Q-0107 AC-8 — and a second description of it here is the
    10	 * transcription defect this repository keeps paying for. What this module claims is the **binary half**: that the CLI reaches that behaviour,
    11	 * and that a refusal is one line and an exit code rather than a stack trace.
    12	 *
    13	 * **Four defects reached this command; Q-0112 repaired the two about `owner` and the other two are
    14	 * preserved** (ground rule 3). Each is pinned in `ticket.test.ts` so a later fix is a deliberate act,
    15	 * and the repaired pair is listed with them because the argument coercion beneath them is unchanged:
    16	 * a reader meeting only the repair would not know that `--owner` still *arrives* as `true`.
    17	 *
    18	 * 1. ~~*`owner` defaults to `process.env.USER`*~~ — **repaired.** `core` reads no environment for an
    19	 *    identity; the default is `unknown` and this module resolves the name. See *"A ticket's owner is
    20	 *    supplied, never guessed"* (2026-09-08).
    21	 * 2. ~~*`--owner` with no following value is the boolean `true`*~~ — **repaired at the write
    22	 *    boundary, not at the flag.** `argv.ts:54` still gives a valueless flag `true`; what changed is
    23	 *    that a boolean is no longer a name, so {@link resolveOwner} ignores it and `create` refuses it
    24	 *    if it ever arrives from anywhere else.
    25	 * 3. *`--intent` with no value reaches `intent.trim()` on a boolean*, and the `catch` below turns
    26	 *    the resulting `TypeError` into `die('intent.trim is not a function')` — a JavaScript message on
    27	 *    a user-facing path.
    28	 * 4. *`--id` with no value is coerced by `String(true)` to `'true'`* and refused as
    29	 *    `not a ticket id: 'true'`. Deliberate in the spike: the coercion is what makes every non-string
    30	 *    reach one grammar rather than several.
    31	 *
    32	 * Why: behaviour preserved from `spike/bin/harness.js:340–352` (Q-0093 AC-2, AC-3).
    33	 */
    34	import path from 'node:path';
    35	
    36	import { configuredUser, loadProject, ProjectNotFoundError, type Backlog } from '@quorum/core';
    37	
    38	import type { FlagValue } from './argv.js';
    39	import { c } from './colour.js';
    40	import { die, dieNoProject } from './fail.js';
    41	import type { CommandHandler } from './main.js';
    42	
    43	/**
    44	 * The backlog this command allocates in, or the spike's sentence and a hard exit where no project
    45	 * is there.
    46	 *
    47	 * Six lines duplicated from `lint.ts` and `runs.ts` rather than shared, and the duplication is
    48	 * forced rather than accepted: a helper module holding it would be a *frame* module naming
    49	 * `loadProject`, which `frame.source.test.ts`'s AC-10 partition forbids.
    50	 *
    51	 * The project is resolved through `loadProject` and nowhere else — ancestor discovery, `--project`
    52	 * and a configured `backlog.path` all included, which is why this module constructs no `backlog/`
    53	 * path of its own. Q-0091 erratum E-6 governs the argument: the spike reads that flag *inside* its
    54	 * own `loadProject`, so passing it here is what keeps `--project` deciding which backlog is written.
    55	 *
    56	 * The message is `core`'s, rendered unaltered — this module composes no recovery advice of its own,
    57	 * which is what keeps one sentence in one place.
    58	 */
    59	function projectOf(project: FlagValue | readonly FlagValue[] | undefined): ReturnType<typeof loadProject> {
    60	  try {
    61	    return loadProject(project as string | undefined);
    62	  } catch (error) {
    63	    if (!(error instanceof ProjectNotFoundError)) throw error;
    64	    return dieNoProject(error.message);
    65	  }
    66	}
    67	
    68	/**
    69	 * Who the ticket belongs to: the flag if it was given a name, then git's, then nobody.
    70	 *
    71	 * **The policy is this surface's and the fact is `core`'s.** `configuredUser` answers what git is
    72	 * configured to call this user and `null` where nothing is configured; deciding that this is the
    73	 * owner, and what to do when there is no answer, is the CLI's. `core` reads no environment for an
    74	 * identity — it stamped `process.env.USER`, the operating-system account, which named a person on
    75	 * nobody's machine and eleven tickets in this backlog. Q-0112.
    76	 *
    77	 * A flag with no value after it is `true` (`argv.ts:54`), which is not a name and is not treated as
    78	 * one; `create` refuses it if it ever arrives, which is the guarantee M3's server inherits.
    79	 */
    80	const resolveOwner = (flag: FlagValue | readonly FlagValue[] | undefined, repoDir: string): string | undefined =>
    81	  (typeof flag === 'string' && flag.trim() ? flag : configuredUser(repoDir) ?? undefined);
    82	
    83	/** The usage line: the argument shape from `spike/bin/harness.js:342`, named `quorum` per Q-0100. */
    84	const USAGE = 'usage: quorum ticket new "<title>" --intent "..." [--id Q-0081]';
    85	
    86	/** Allocate one ticket folder and print where it landed. */
    87	export const ticket: CommandHandler = ({ rest, flags }) => {
    88	  const { backlog, repoDir } = projectOf(flags.project);
    89	  if (rest[0] !== 'new') die(USAGE);
    90	  const title = rest[1];
    91	  if (!title) die('title required');
    92	  // An id the backlog refuses to allocate, or a folder it refuses to overwrite, is a sentence and an
    93	  // exit code — not the Node stack `dieOnUnexpected` would print. The four argument expressions are
    94	  // the spike's, preserved rather than paraphrased: three of them are the defects above.
    95	  //
    96	  // The success line is outside the `try`, where the spike puts it: a throw from the printing is a
    97	  // defect and must stay visible as one rather than being reported as a refusal the backlog made.
    98	  let created: ReturnType<Backlog['create']>;
    99	  try {
   100	    created = backlog.create({
   101	      title,
   102	      intent: (flags.intent ?? title) as string,
   103	      owner: resolveOwner(flags.owner, repoDir),
   104	      id: flags.id === undefined ? undefined : String(flags.id),
   105	    });
   106	  } catch (error) {
   107	    return die((error as Error).message);
   108	  }
   109	  console.log(`${c.green('✓')} ${created.meta.id} created at ${path.relative(process.cwd(), created.dir)} (stage: draft)`);
   110	};
   170	      process.chdir(orphan);
   171	      refusal(await invoke(['ticket', 'new', 'Nowhere']), 'no harness/harness.yaml found');
   172	    } finally {
   173	      process.chdir(project);
   174	      fs.rmSync(orphan, { recursive: true, force: true });
   175	    }
   176	  });
   177	
   178	  test('while `--project` at a directory that is no project raises, which is preserved', async () => {
   179	    // Why: preserved — `loadProject(dir)` consults `findProject` only when `dir` is absent, so an
   180	    // explicit `--project` is resolved and its `harness/harness.yaml` opened, and an absent one is
   181	    // an `ENOENT` rather than the sentence above. `lint.test.ts` pins the identical behaviour for
   182	    // Q-0091's command; both are the spike's `flags.project ? path.resolve(…) : findProject()`.
   183	    const orphan = fs.realpathSync(fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'quorum-cli-ticket-noconfig-')));
   184	    try {
   185	      await expect(invoke(['ticket', 'new', 'Nowhere', '--project', orphan])).rejects.toThrow(/ENOENT/);
   186	    } finally {
   187	      fs.rmSync(orphan, { recursive: true, force: true });
   188	    }
   189	  });
   190	});

   192	describe('AC-3 — four preserved defects, demonstrated and pinned rather than repaired', () => {
   193	  test('(a) Q-0112 — `--owner` with no value is not a name, so it falls through to git', async () => {
   194	    // The defect this pinned: `argv.ts:54` gives a flag the value `true` when the next token is
   195	    // another flag or absent, and `create`'s destructuring default fired only on `undefined`, so
   196	    // `owner: true` reached the frontmatter — a value `ticketSchema` REFUSES, written by the
   197	    // product's own command. Two lines apart in two packages, and neither wrong on its own.
   198	    //
   199	    // Closed at the write boundary rather than at the flag: `argv`'s coercion is unchanged and
   200	    // still gives `true`, and what changed is that a boolean is no longer a name. `resolveOwner`
   201	    // therefore ignores it and asks git, which is why this now reads a person.
   202	    // Git's identity is CONTROLLED rather than read, through an isolated config file: this fixture
   203	    // is not a repository, so git answers from the GLOBAL file — and an assertion that merely
   204	    // refused `true` would be satisfied by `unknown`, would not show the fall-through it claims,
   205	    // and would have a verdict that is a property of the machine ("A test's verdict is a property of
   206	    // the commit, not of the checkout or the account", 2026-08-30). This is the claim.
   207	    const config = path.join(fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'q0112-cfg-')), 'gitconfig');
   208	    fs.writeFileSync(config, '[user]\n\tname = Grace Hopper\n');
   209	    vi.stubEnv('GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL', config);
   210	    vi.stubEnv('GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM', '/dev/null');
   211	    try {
   212	      expect((await invoke(['ticket', 'new', 'Owned', '--owner'])).exitCode).toBe(SUCCESS);
   213	      expect(field('T-0001-owned', 'owner'), 'the boolean was taken as a name, or git was not asked')
   214	        .toBe('Grace Hopper');
   215	    } finally {
   216	      vi.unstubAllEnvs();
   217	    }
   218	  });
   219	
   220	  test('(b) `--intent` with no value reports a JavaScript message on a user-facing path', async () => {
   221	    // Why: preserved defect — the boolean reaches `intent.trim()` inside `create`, and the `catch`
   222	    // turns the `TypeError` into a refusal whose sentence is the engine's rather than the product's.
   223	    refusal(await invoke(['ticket', 'new', 'Intentless', '--intent']), 'intent.trim is not a function');
   224	  });
   225	
   226	  test('and that failure leaves an empty ticket folder behind, which is measured and not fixed here', async () => {
   227	    // **Found by running (b) rather than by reading it, and reported rather than repaired.**
   228	    // `Backlog.create` performs its three checks before creating anything and then does
   229	    // `mkdirSync(dir)` *before* evaluating `intent.trim()` — `backlog.ts:206-209`, and
   230	    // `spike/src/backlog.js:128-131` identically — so a bad `--intent` allocates the directory and
   231	    // throws on the next expression, leaving a folder with no `ticket.md` in it. The consequence is
   232	    // the one that matters: that folder is not a ticket to `list()`, and it *is* a taken id to
   233	    // `create()`, so the next allocation refuses `T-0001` and a `ticket new` with a good intent
   234	    // cannot use the id its own failed predecessor consumed.
   235	    //
   236	    // It is in both trees and it is `core`'s, so ground rule 3 makes it a report and not a fix; the
   237	    // method's own doc comment says a refusal leaves the backlog byte for byte as it was, which is
   238	    // true of its three *checks* and not of an argument that is the wrong type. Pinned so a later
   239	    // repair is a deliberate act and so the claim is executable rather than prose.
   240	    refusal(await invoke(['ticket', 'new', 'Intentless', '--intent']), 'intent.trim is not a function');
   241	    expect(folders()).toStrictEqual(['T-0001-intentless']);
   242	    expect(fs.readdirSync(path.join(project, 'backlog', 'T-0001-intentless')), 'the folder holds a ticket after all')
   243	      .toStrictEqual([]);
   244	    refusal(
   245	      await invoke(['ticket', 'new', 'Intentless', '--intent', 'a real intent']),
   246	      'ticket folder already exists: T-0001-intentless',
   247	    );
   248	  });
   249	
   250	  test('(c) `--id` with no value is coerced to the string `true` and refused as a bad id', async () => {
   251	    // Why: preserved — `String(flags.id)` is what makes every non-string reach one grammar rather
   252	    // than several, and the resulting message is the grammar's own. Deliberate in the spike.
   253	    refusal(await invoke(['ticket', 'new', 'Idless', '--intent', 'i', '--id']), "not a ticket id: 'true'");
   254	  });
   255	
   256	  test('(d) Q-0112 — `$USER` is ignored, and an unattributed ticket says so', async () => {
   257	    // The defect this pinned: `Backlog.create` defaulted `owner` to `process.env.USER`, the
   258	    // operating-system account of whoever ran the command — on a shared machine, in CI, in a
   259	    // container, and in every worktree a flow creates. Eleven tickets in this backlog were stamped
   260	    // that way and four hand corrections never reached the code.
   261	    //
   262	    // `core` now reads no environment for an identity. **The stub is kept rather than removed**,
   263	    // which is what makes this demonstrate the ignoring instead of merely not exercising it — and
   264	    // it stays controlled and restored for the reason it always was: a verdict taken from `$USER`
   265	    // would be a property of the machine ("A test's verdict is a property of the commit, not of the
   266	    // checkout or the account", 2026-08-30).
   267	    const saved = process.env.USER;
   268	    try {
   269	      process.env.USER = 'somebody-else';
   270	      expect((await invoke(['ticket', 'new', 'Stamped', '--intent', 'i'])).exitCode).toBe(SUCCESS);
   271	      expect(field('T-0001-stamped', 'owner'), '$USER reached the frontmatter').not.toBe('somebody-else');
   272	    } finally {
   273	      if (saved === undefined) delete process.env.USER;
   274	      else process.env.USER = saved;
   275	    }
   276	  });
   277	
   278	  test('and where git can name nobody either, the ticket is `unknown` rather than a guess', () => {
   279	    // The honest end of the chain, asserted on `core` directly because it is `core`'s answer: the
   280	    // CLI supplies a name or omits it, and an omitted owner is an admission and not an account.
   281	    const backlog = new Backlog(fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'q0112-')));
   282	    expect(backlog.create({ title: 'Nobody', intent: 'i' }).meta.owner).toBe('unknown');
   283	  });
   284	
   285	  test('and a non-string owner is refused by name rather than written', () => {
   286	    // The guarantee M3's server inherits: `ticketSchema` types `owner` as a string, and this is the
   287	    // write boundary. A request body carrying `true` is refused here, not at each surface.
   288	    const backlog = new Backlog(fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'q0112-')));
   289	    expect(() => backlog.create({ title: 'Bad', intent: 'i', owner: true as unknown as string }))
   290	      .toThrow(/not an owner: true/);
   291	    expect(() => backlog.create({ title: 'Blank', intent: 'i', owner: '   ' }))
   292	      .toThrow(/not an owner/);
   293	  });
   294	
   295	  test('and an explicit --owner is what the flag is for, so the default is a default and not a rule', async () => {
   296	    // The discriminating half: without it the two rows above would hold for an implementation that
   297	    // ignored `--owner` entirely and always wrote the environment's answer.
   298	    const saved = process.env.USER;
   299	    try {
   300	      process.env.USER = 'somebody-else';
   301	      expect((await invoke(['ticket', 'new', 'Assigned', '--intent', 'i', '--owner', 'ruud'])).exitCode).toBe(SUCCESS);
   302	      expect(field('T-0001-assigned', 'owner')).toBe('ruud');
   303	    } finally {
   304	      if (saved === undefined) delete process.env.USER;
   305	      else process.env.USER = saved;
   306	    }
   307	  });
   308	});
   160	    if (!ids.length) throw new Error(unreadableBacklog(tickets));
   161	
   162	    const counts = new Map<string, number>();
   163	    for (const { prefix } of ids) counts.set(prefix, (counts.get(prefix) ?? 0) + 1);
   164	    if (counts.size > 1) throw new Error(mixedPrefixes(counts));
   165	
   166	    const { prefix } = ids[0];
   167	    const highest = Math.max(...ids.map((id) => id.number));
   168	    const next = `${prefix}-${String(highest + 1).padStart(4, '0')}`;
   169	    // The grammar is the oracle rather than a second spelling of "four digits": one past the last
   170	    // id a backlog can hold is exactly the string it rejects.
   171	    if (!parseTicketId(next)) throw new Error(exhaustedPrefix(prefix, highest, next));
   172	    return next;
   173	  }
   174	
   175	  /**
   176	   * Allocate a ticket folder and write its `ticket.md`. The frontmatter key order below is the
   177	   * file's key order: not alphabetised, not sorted, not "tidied".
   178	   *
   179	   * It refuses a taken id and an occupied folder rather than allocating around either: allocating
   180	   * around one papers over an allocator that produced an id already in use, which is the state this
   181	   * refusal exists to make impossible. Every check runs before anything is created, and the ticket
   182	   * directory is then created exclusively, so a refusal leaves the backlog byte for byte as it was.
   183	   *
   184	   * Why: `branch` is a NAME and nothing here creates the ref, which is half of why the chore flow
   185	   * cannot run on a ticket's first pass — Q-0038 carries that (register row 19).
   186	   *
   187	   * @throws {Error} when `id` is not of the form `<PREFIX>-nnnn`, when the id already belongs to a
   188	   *   folder, or when the target folder exists. `nextId`'s refusals reach the caller unchanged.
   189	   */
   190	  create({ title, intent, owner = UNATTRIBUTED, repos = [], id: given }: NewTicket): TicketRecord {
   191	    // `ticketSchema` types `owner` as a string, and this is the write boundary — a caller that hands
   192	    // over a boolean, which `--owner` with no value does, would otherwise put a value in the backlog
   193	    // that the product's own schema refuses. Refused here rather than at each surface, so the same
   194	    // guarantee covers M3's server taking an owner from a request body. Q-0112.
   195	    if (typeof owner !== 'string' || !owner.trim()) throw new Error(notAnOwner(owner));
   196	    if (given !== undefined && !parseTicketId(given)) throw new Error(notATicketId(given));
   197	    const id = given ?? this.nextId();
   198	    const slug = title.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, '-').replace(/(^-|-$)/g, '').slice(0, 40);
   199	    const folder = `${id}-${slug}`;
   200	    const dir = path.join(this.root, folder);
   201	    const entries = fs.existsSync(this.root) ? fs.readdirSync(this.root) : [];
   202	    if (entries.includes(folder)) throw new Error(`ticket folder already exists: ${folder}`);
   203	    // The same resolution dirOf uses, so "the id already belongs to a folder" means what a reader
   204	    // of this backlog would mean by it — a differing slug included.
   205	    const taken = entries.find((name) => name === id || name.startsWith(`${id}-`));
   206	    if (taken !== undefined) throw new Error(`ticket id already taken: ${id} already belongs to ${taken}`);
   207	    // Two jobs, deliberately two calls: the root is created if it is missing, and the ticket
   208	    // directory is created EXCLUSIVELY, so `ticket.md` is opened only once this call owns the
   209	    // folder. One `recursive: true` over the whole path would do both and silently accept an
   210	    // existing ticket folder, which is the overwrite (Q-0080 AC-5, AC-6).
   211	    fs.mkdirSync(this.root, { recursive: true });
   212	    fs.mkdirSync(dir);
   213	    const ticket: TicketRecord = {
   214	      dir, folder: path.basename(dir), body: intent.trim() + '\n',
   215	      meta: {
   216	        id, title, stage: 'draft', owner, repos,
   217	        branch: integrationBranch(id), priority: 'p2',
   218	        created: new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10),
   219	        iterations: {}, history: [],
   220	      },
   221	    };
   222	    this.write(ticket);
   223	    return ticket;
   224	  }
   225	
   226	  /**
   227	   * Read files inside the ticket folder, with the simple glob every flow's `input.backlog` uses —
   228	   * so this decides what an adapter is invoked with, which the charter calls externally observable.
   229	   * The syntax is deliberately narrow and is not widened: only `*` is a wildcard, and every other
   230	   * regex metacharacter is escaped and matches literally. A pattern ending in `/` walks that
   231	   * subtree and preserves the walk's own filesystem order; everything else is sorted by basename.
   232	   */
   233	  readFiles(ticket: TicketRecord, pattern: string): TicketFile[] {
   234	    const dir = path.dirname(path.join(ticket.dir, pattern));
   235	    const base = path.basename(pattern);
   236	    if (!fs.existsSync(dir)) return [];
   237	    if (pattern.endsWith('/')) {
   238	      return walk(path.join(ticket.dir, pattern)).map((f) => ({ rel: path.relative(ticket.dir, f), text: fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8') }));
   239	    }
   240	    const re = new RegExp('^' + base.replace(/[.+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&').replace(/\*/g, '.*') + '$');
   241	    return fs.readdirSync(dir).filter((n) => re.test(n)).sort()
   242	      .map((n) => ({ rel: path.relative(ticket.dir, path.join(dir, n)), text: fs.readFileSync(path.join(dir, n), 'utf8') }));
   243	  }
   244	
   245	  /** Write a file inside the ticket folder, creating parents, and return its absolute path. */
   246	  writeFile(ticket: TicketRecord, rel: string, text: string): string {
   247	    const abs = path.join(ticket.dir, rel);
   248	    fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(abs), { recursive: true });
   249	    fs.writeFileSync(abs, text.endsWith('\n') ? text : text + '\n');
   250	    return abs;
   251	  }
   252	
   253	  /** Append one line to the ticket's run log. Append-only: an existing line is never rewritten. */
   254	  log(ticket: TicketRecord, line: string): void {
   255	    fs.appendFileSync(path.join(ticket.dir, RUNS_LOG_FILE), `${new Date().toISOString()} ${line}\n`);
   256	  }
   257	}

   259	/**
   260	 * What every allocation refusal ends with. A refusal that names no way forward reads as a wall,
   261	 * and `--id` is the way past all three of them.
   262	 */
   263	const ACTION = 'pass --id <ID> or reconcile the backlog';
   264	
   265	/** How the grammar is described to whoever has to fix an id by hand. */
   266	const FORM = '<PREFIX>-nnnn';
   267	
   268	/** At most this many ids are quoted back, so a hundred-ticket backlog still prints one line. */
   269	const SAMPLE = 3;
   270	
   271	/**
   272	 * A control character in a value this message quotes back, rendered as an escape rather than
   273	 * written through.
   274	 *
   275	 * Why: the value is attacker-controlled — it is whatever `--id` was given — and the message is one
   276	 * line by contract. A newline splits it into three, and the second reads like harness output; an
   277	 * ANSI escape colours the terminal. Reported as a nit by Q-0080's reviewer and reproduced before
   278	 * it was believed.
   279	 */
   280	const printable = (value: string): string =>
   281	  // eslint-disable-next-line no-control-regex -- the class being escaped is exactly the control range
   282	  value.replace(/[\u0000-\u001f\u007f]/g, (c) => `\\x${c.codePointAt(0)!.toString(16).padStart(2, '0')}`);
   283	
   284	/**
   285	 * What a ticket carries when nobody said who it belongs to.
   286	 *
   287	 * `core` does not read the environment for an identity. `process.env.USER` stood here and named the
   288	 * operating-system account rather than a person — on a shared machine, in CI, in a container and in
   289	 * every worktree a flow creates — and the backlog carried eleven tickets stamped that way, corrected
   290	 * by hand four times without the correction ever reaching the code. An admission is honest where a
   291	 * guess is not; who a ticket belongs to is the surface's to supply. Q-0112.
   292	 */
   293	const UNATTRIBUTED = 'unknown';
   294	
   295	/**
   296	 * An owner that is not a name: whatever a caller handed over where a string was required.
   297	 *
   298	 * The condition and no remedy — which surface asked, and what it should tell its user to do about
   299	 * it, is not knowable here. See *"A `core` error names the condition; the remedy belongs to the
   300	 * surface"* (2026-09-07).
   301	 */
   302	const notAnOwner = (given: unknown): string =>
   303	  `not an owner: ${typeof given === 'string' ? `'${printable(given)}'` : String(given)} — an owner is a non-empty name`;
   304	
   305	/** An `--id` the grammar does not recognise, named with the shape it should have had. */
   306	const notATicketId = (given: string): string =>
   307	  `not a ticket id: '${printable(given)}' — an id is ${FORM}, like Q-0081`;
   308	
   309	/**
   310	 * Tickets are there and not one of their ids parses. Sorted before it is cut, so the sample is the
   311	 * same sentence whatever order the filesystem listed the folders in.
   312	 */
   313	function unreadableBacklog(tickets: readonly TicketRecord[]): string {
   314	  const seen = [...new Set(tickets.map((t) => String(t.meta.id)))].sort();
   315	  const quoted = seen.slice(0, SAMPLE).map((id) => `'${id}'`).join(', ');

    12	 * — the one copy both trees assert over. A second description of them in this package would be free
    13	 * to drift from the first, which is the defect this repository keeps finding. What is claimed here

    50	const folders = (dir: string = project): string[] => fs.readdirSync(path.join(dir, 'backlog')).sort();

    55	
    56	/** What a refusal must look like whatever it is refusing: one line, exit 1, and no stack. */
    57	function refusal(result: Invocation, sentence: string): void {
    58	  expect(result.exitCode).toBe(ERROR);
    59	  expect(result.hard, 'a refusal that did not stop the command').toBe(true);
    60	  expect(plain(result.stderr)).toContain(sentence);
    61	  expect(plain(result.stderr).trimEnd().split('\n'), `not one line: ${result.stderr}`).toHaveLength(1);
    62	  expect(result.stderr.includes('\n    at '), 'a stack trace tells an adopter the product crashed').toBe(false);
    63	}
    64	
    65	describe('AC-2 — the allocation is reached through the CLI, and the table\'s binary half runs', () => {

    67	    // The same title three times, which is what makes the folder assertion mean something: the slug

   256	  test('(d) Q-0112 — `$USER` is ignored, and an unattributed ticket says so', async () => {
   257	    // The defect this pinned: `Backlog.create` defaulted `owner` to `process.env.USER`, the
   258	    // operating-system account of whoever ran the command — on a shared machine, in CI, in a
   259	    // container, and in every worktree a flow creates. Eleven tickets in this backlog were stamped
   260	    // that way and four hand corrections never reached the code.
   261	    //
   262	    // `core` now reads no environment for an identity. **The stub is kept rather than removed**,
   263	    // which is what makes this demonstrate the ignoring instead of merely not exercising it — and
   264	    // it stays controlled and restored for the reason it always was: a verdict taken from `$USER`
   265	    // would be a property of the machine ("A test's verdict is a property of the commit, not of the
   266	    // checkout or the account", 2026-08-30).
   267	    const saved = process.env.USER;
   268	    try {
   269	      process.env.USER = 'somebody-else';
   270	      expect((await invoke(['ticket', 'new', 'Stamped', '--intent', 'i'])).exitCode).toBe(SUCCESS);
   271	      expect(field('T-0001-stamped', 'owner'), '$USER reached the frontmatter').not.toBe('somebody-else');
   272	    } finally {
   273	      if (saved === undefined) delete process.env.USER;
   274	      else process.env.USER = saved;
   275	    }
   276	  });

   280	    // CLI supplies a name or omits it, and an omitted owner is an admission and not an account.
   281	    const backlog = new Backlog(fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'q0112-')));

   287	    // write boundary. A request body carrying `true` is refused here, not at each surface.
   288	    const backlog = new Backlog(fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'q0112-')));

   290	      .toThrow(/not an owner: true/);

     1	// Q-0042: worktrees, ancestry and containment, asserted against real git.
     2	//
     3	// The independent witness here is git itself, not the spike's suite — both suites can be green
     4	// over a wrong port (harness/port-charter.md §2), because a test ported alongside a mis-ported
     5	// module agrees with it. So every case below builds the repository, the topology and the shallow
     6	// state it asserts, and no case asserts the containment state of a branch in THIS repository.
     7	import fs from 'node:fs';
     8	import path from 'node:path';

    14	import {
    15	  ancestry, configuredUser, containment, emptyRangeEvidence, ensureExcluded, ensureWorktree, mergeBase, pushLag,
    16	  removeWorktree, shallowState, shortSha,
    17	} from './git.js';

    25	/** `feature` forked before `main` moved on: feature is contained in main, main is not in feature. */
    26	function forked(): string {
    27	  const dir = repo();
    28	  git(dir, 'branch', 'feature');
    29	  commit(dir, 'main moves on');
    30	  return dir;
    31	}
    32	
    33	const TICKET_BRANCH = 'harness/T-1/integration';
    34	
    35	/** A repository whose ticket branch exists and is contained in `main`. */
    36	function withTicketBranch(): string {
    37	  const dir = repo();
    38	  git(dir, 'branch', TICKET_BRANCH);
    39	  return dir;
    40	}
