    42	describe('AC-2 — ancestry selects its state from git\'s exit code and from nothing else', () => {
    43	  test('exit 0 is contained, and every result carries all four keys', () => {
    44	    expect(ancestry(forked(), 'feature', 'main')).toStrictEqual({
    45	      state: 'contained',
    46	      reason: null,
    47	      detail: null,
    48	      command: 'git merge-base --is-ancestor feature main',
    49	    });
    50	  });
    51	
    52	  test('exit 0 stays contained whatever the shallow state — found history is real', () => {
    53	    const dir = forked();
    54	    for (const shallow of [true, false, null] as const) {
    55	      expect(ancestry(dir, 'feature', 'main', { shallow }).state, `shallow: ${shallow}`).toBe('contained');
    56	    }
    57	  });
    58	
    59	  test('exit 1 in a repository known not to be shallow is provably not contained', () => {
    60	    expect(ancestry(forked(), 'main', 'feature')).toStrictEqual({
    61	      state: 'not-contained',
    62	      reason: null,
    63	      detail: null,
    64	      command: 'git merge-base --is-ancestor main feature',
    65	    });
    66	  });
    67	
    68	  test('exit 1 in a shallow repository is indeterminate — absent history cannot disprove ancestry', () => {
    69	    expect(ancestry(forked(), 'main', 'feature', { shallow: true })).toStrictEqual({
    70	      state: 'indeterminate',
    71	      reason: 'shallow clone',
    72	      detail: null,
    73	      command: 'git merge-base --is-ancestor main feature',
    74	    });
    75	  });
    76	
    77	  test('exit 1 with an unanswered shallow probe is indeterminate, and carries the probe\'s detail', () => {
    78	    const result = ancestry(forked(), 'main', 'feature', {
    79	      shallow: null,
    80	      shallowDetail: 'fatal: not a git repository',
    81	    });
    82	    expect(result.state).toBe('indeterminate');
    83	    expect(result.reason).toBe('shallow state unknown');
    84	    expect(result.detail).toBe('fatal: not a git repository');
    85	  });
    86	
    87	  test('any other exit, and a git that could not answer at all, are indeterminate — never not-contained', () => {
    88	    for (const [what, result] of [
    89	      ['a ref that does not resolve', ancestry(forked(), 'no/such/ref', 'main')],
    90	      ['a directory that is not a repository', ancestry(notARepo(), 'main', 'main')],
    91	    ] as const) {
    92	      expect(result.state, what).toBe('indeterminate');
    93	      expect(result.reason, what).toBe('git failed');
    94	      expect(result.detail, what).toBeTruthy();
    95	      expect(String(result.detail), what).not.toContain('\n');
    96	      expect(result.command, what).toContain('merge-base --is-ancestor');
    97	    }
    98	  });
    99	
   100	  test('detail is one line of at most 200 characters', () => {
   101	    const detail = ancestry(forked(), 'x'.repeat(400), 'main').detail;
   102	    expect(detail).toBeTruthy();
   103	    expect(String(detail).length).toBeLessThanOrEqual(200);
   104	  });
   105	
   106	  test('detail decides nothing: two different failures give the same state and reason', () => {
   107	    const missingRef = ancestry(forked(), 'no/such/ref', 'main');
   108	    const outside = ancestry(notARepo(), 'main', 'main');
   109	    expect(missingRef.detail).not.toBe(outside.detail);
   110	    expect([missingRef.state, missingRef.reason]).toEqual([outside.state, outside.reason]);
   111	  });
   112	});

   114	describe('AC-3 — shallowState is three-valued and says when it could not ask', () => {
   115	  test('an ordinary repository reports false', () => {
   116	    expect(shallowState(repo())).toStrictEqual({ shallow: false, detail: null });
   117	  });
   118	
   119	  test('a genuinely shallow clone reports true', () => {
   120	    expect(shallowState(shallowCloneOf(forked()))).toStrictEqual({ shallow: true, detail: null });
   900	  });
   901	
   902	  test('it costs seven spawns, and that is constant in the number of tickets', () => {
   903	    // The measured half of `containment`'s revised budget sentence: 2n + 3 for the board's rows,
   904	    // plus this, which does not move when n does. Pinned exactly rather than bounded, so that a
   905	    // probe added or removed moves the number the JSDoc states instead of hiding inside a ceiling
   906	    // — the budget is a sentence somebody reads, and a sentence nothing checks goes stale.
   907	    const dir = tracking({ local: 2 });
   908	    const { calls } = counting(() => pushLag(dir, 'trunk'));
   909	    expect(calls, 'the longest path costs what the JSDoc says it costs').toBe(7);
   910	    expect(counting(() => pushLag(repo(), 'main')).calls,
   911	      'the cheap answers must not cost more than the expensive one').toBeLessThanOrEqual(7);
   912	  });
   913	});

   915	describe('Q-0112 — configuredUser answers what git is configured to call this user, and nothing else', () => {
   916	  /** A repository whose `user.name` is exactly `name`; `repo()` sets none of its own. */
   917	  function named(name: string): string {
   918	    const dir = repo();
   919	    git(dir, 'config', 'user.name', name);
   920	    return dir;
   921	  }
   922	
   923	  test('a configured name is returned as it is written', () => {
   924	    expect(configuredUser(named('Ada Lovelace'))).toBe('Ada Lovelace');
   925	  });
   926	
   927	  test('surrounding whitespace is trimmed, because a name is what git holds and not how it is spaced', () => {
   928	    expect(configuredUser(named('  Ada  '))).toBe('Ada');
   929	  });
   930	
   931	  test('a name that is only whitespace is NOT a name, and reads as unconfigured', () => {
   932	    // The clause `'' || null` would miss: git stores the value, `--get` returns it, and a caller
   933	    // that took it would attribute a ticket to a blank string.
   934	    expect(configuredUser(named('   '))).toBeNull();
   935	  });
   936	
   937	  /**
   938	   * The same isolation `.github/scripts/git-identity-sweep.sh` uses, and for the same reason.
   939	   *
   940	   * Unsetting `user.name` in the repository is NOT enough — `git config user.name` falls back to the
   941	   * global file, so the first draft of the two tests below asserted `null` and got this developer's
   942	   * own name. That is the behaviour we want (git's answer, wherever git found it) and a test premise
   943	   * that was simply wrong, which running it is what caught.
   944	   */
   945	  function withNoConfiguredIdentity<T>(body: () => T): T {
   946	    vi.stubEnv('GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL', path.join(tempDir('q0112-absent-'), 'nothing-here'));
   947	    vi.stubEnv('GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM', '/dev/null');
   948	    try { return body(); } finally { vi.unstubAllEnvs(); }
   949	  }
   950	
   951	  test('a name configured nowhere at all is null rather than a fallback', () => {
   952	    const dir = repo();
   953	    expect(withNoConfiguredIdentity(() => configuredUser(dir))).toBeNull();
   954	  });
   955	
   956	  test('and a directory that is not a repository is null too, rather than throwing', () => {
   957	    // `ticket.ts` calls this on the way to writing a ticket, so a throw here would turn "git has no
   958	    // opinion" into a crash on the command's own path. Isolated for the same reason: outside a
   959	    // repository git still answers from the global file.
   960	    expect(withNoConfiguredIdentity(() => configuredUser(notARepo()))).toBeNull();
   961	  });
   962	});

.claude/rules/engineering.md
.claude/rules/docs-and-decisions.md
.claude/rules/product-boundaries.md
     1	# Docs and decisions
     2	
     3	- The decisions are append-only. Each is one file in `docs/decisions/`, named `NNN-slug.md`, opening `# <title> — <YYYY-MM-DD>` and carrying **Decision**, **Alternatives considered**, **Why**; `docs/DECISIONS.md` is the index and gains one line per entry. A landed entry is never edited — reversing one is a new entry that names the old one. Cite an entry by its title and date, never by its file name or number.
     4	- **An entry's date is the date it takes its place in the index**, not the date the choice was made; where the two differ the body says when it was decided. `packages/shared/src/docs.test.ts` enforces it — the index's dates never go backwards — so a back-dated entry turns the suite red. See *"An entry's date is the date it takes its place in the index"* (2026-09-01).
     5	- `docs/GLOSSARY.md` is the vocabulary. When you need a new term, add it there before using it in a second file. Do not introduce synonyms for existing terms.
     6	- The numbered docs (01–06) are living documents; edit in place and bump the status line at the top with the date and what changed.
     7	- When code and docs disagree, the docs are wrong until a DECISIONS.md entry says otherwise — fix the docs in the same PR.
     8	- Milestone ends with a DECISIONS.md entry: what was learned, what changed.
     9	- Write prose, not bullet walls, in the numbered docs; bullets are fine in rules and plans.

[35m[3mcodex[0m[0m
major docs/decisions/084-a-tickets-owner-is-supplied-never-guessed.md:34 — The entry claims the `Backlog.create` write-boundary check “covers the CLI flag today,” but `resolveOwner` consumes the boolean and substitutes Git’s name or `undefined`; the invalid value never reaches that check. Distinguish the CLI’s surface-level normalization from the independent write-boundary defense so this append-only architectural rationale accurately describes the implementation.

major packages/cli/src/ticket.test.ts:256 — This test neither isolates Git configuration nor proves that the ticket is unattributed. An ambient global `user.name` determines the stored owner, and if it equals `somebody-else` the test fails, making the verdict machine-dependent. Isolate global/system Git configuration and assert `owner: unknown`, or rename the test to the narrower `$USER` claim and control Git to a distinct expected identity.

nit packages/cli/src/ticket.test.ts:192 — The enclosing suite still says all four defects are “preserved” and “not repaired,” directly contradicting its two Q-0112 repair tests. Update the suite description to distinguish the two repaired owner defects from the two preserved argument defects.

nit packages/cli/src/board.test.ts:90 — The comment still describes `Backlog.create` as defaulting the owner to the account, which is no longer current behavior. Update it to reflect the `unknown` core default and CLI-owned resolution.
